What a run is. One invocation of the framework on one design with one mode: here raygentop with comb_sop_2_18_mode.

What happened. 3 rewrites measured (Pass 1, Pass 2, Pass 3); kept: Pass 2 (the lowest area x delay, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.60%). Loop stopped by: no gain beyond noise (gain -14.00% against Pass 2, min-gain 0.046 (baseline seed_figures (area x delay over the entry's seed runs))).

Result. Area x delay -0.02% against the unmodified design on the same layout (koios_extra_small, 90 x 90): 2.30579e+07 vs 2.30616e+07 (baseline: median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them)); verification: not recorded in the run record.

Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `comb_sop_2_18` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
- input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
- output ports: result[64]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: none; the block is combinational, and `result` is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

id
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348

bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dgul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dgvl}, result → resg
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dbvl}, result → resb
  • drul/drvl/dgul/dgvl/dbul/dbvl are new 7-bit stage-1 registers holding ru-rw, rv-rw, gu-gw, gv-gw, bu-bw, bv-bw. No product-term port is tied to 0 (all four are used on every block).

PLAN:

Why all nine products fit in three blocks. All of i1r…i3b are internal regs read only by the three assign statements at lines 2884–2886 (checked: no other reader in the file; the only instantiation of bilinearintrp is line 2012 and it connects only r, g, b). Each output is 7 bits, so only the sum mod 2^7 is visible. Line 2862 gives wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v at the same edge as ul <= u and vl <= v (lines 2863–2864), so wl ≡ -(1 + ul + vl) (mod 128) exactly, for all values, including the mod-256 wrap. Therefore

r = ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl            (mod 128)
  = ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl   (mod 128)

and the same for g and b. That is two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 per colour — plus a 7-bit correction term - rwl outside the block. This is exact arithmetic, not an approximation: no ASSUMES line is needed.

Edit to make (replace the declarations at lines 2794–2816, the always block body at 2860–2883, and the assigns at 2884–2886; leave the u/v case statement at 2818–2858 and the port list untouched):

verilog
        reg[7:0] u;
        reg[7:0] v;
        reg[7:0] ul;
        reg[7:0] vl;
        reg[6:0] rwl, gwl, bwl;          // stage 1 of the rw/gw/bw delay
        reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;       // stage 2, aligns with the block result
        reg[6:0] drul, drvl, dgul, dgvl, dbul, dbvl;
        reg[6:0] sr, sg, sb;             // registered block results, low 7 bits
        wire[63:0] resr, resg, resb;

        comb_sop_2_18 sop_r (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, drul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, drvl}), .result(resr));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_g (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dgul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dgvl}), .result(resg));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_b (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dbul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dbvl}), .result(resb));

        // (the u/v case statement of lines 2818-2858 stays exactly as it is)

        always @(posedge clk)
        begin
           ul <= u;
           vl <= v;
           rwl <= rw;   gwl <= gw;   bwl <= bw;
           rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl;
           drul <= ru - rw;   drvl <= rv - rw;
           dgul <= gu - gw;   dgvl <= gv - gw;
           dbul <= bu - bw;   dbvl <= bv - bw;
           sr <= resr[6:0];
           sg <= resg[6:0];
           sb <= resb[6:0];
        end

        assign r = sr - rwl2;
        assign g = sg - gwl2;
        assign b = sb - bwl2;

Deleted as dead: wl, i1r–i3b, and rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (their only readers were the nine multiplies). Keep the module header, port list, and the u/v combinational case block byte-for-byte.

Width/wrap check. ul ≤ 255 (8 bits ≤ ax's 18, zero-extended); drul ≤ 127 (7 bits ≤ ay's 19, zero-extended). Each product ≤ 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^64, so the block never wraps and resr[6:0] is the true sum mod 128. sr - rwl2 is 7-bit unsigned arithmetic, wrapping mod 128, which is what the identity requires. The original assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) evaluates in 15 bits and truncates to 7, i.e. also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Cycle alignment. Original: r(t) = ul(t-1)*rul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*rvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*rwl(t-1), i.e. a function of the module inputs at t-2. New: sr(t) = resr(t-1) = ul(t-1)*drul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*drvl(t-1) with drul(t-1) = (ru-rw)(t-2), and rwl2(t) = rw(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle.

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,rul,rvl,wl,rwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input add → output. Two register stages from operand to result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,drul,drvl, and the rwl leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 1 register (sr/sg/sb, and rwl2 in parallel) → combinational 7-bit subtract → output. Still two register stages from operand to result; the product register became the sum register, and the operand subtracts were pushed in front of the stage-1 register so the block sees register outputs directly (path ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 ns).

Candidates left unconverted: none. The six ul*/vl* products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) map directly onto the blocks' two product terms. The three wl* products (2876, 2879, 2882) are not computed as multiplies any more: substituting wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) folds each one into its sum's other two products (as the -rwl offset in the operands) plus the single 7-bit - rwl2 correction outside the block, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Reset. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none (reset policy: none). The added registers drul…dbvl, rwl2/gwl2/bwl2, sr/sg/sb start unknown; all of them are written on the first posedge and the compare only begins after the 8-cycle lead-in, during which the inputs are held at zero, so r=g=b=0 matches the original from the first compared cycle onward. Nothing relies on their initial value.

Area/delay note for the editor (no action needed): 3 DSP tiles are added (761 337 MWTA) and nine 8×7 soft multipliers plus nine 15-bit and six 7-bit registers are removed; the output path shortens from a 15-bit 3-input add to a 7-bit subtract.

output
1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

1	// tb_equiv_bilin.v: equivalence testbench for raygentop's bilinearintrp, generated by verify/equiv/gen_tb.py
2	// on 2026-10-22; do not edit, regenerate (the command is in make_testbenches.sh):
3	//   gen_tb.py --source /home/samu/dev/llm-hardblocks/vtr-verilog-to-routing/vtr_flow/benchmarks/verilog/raygentop.v --module bilinearintrp --ref bilin_ref --design raygentop --out tb_equiv_bilin.v --tasks tasks_raygentop_bilin.vh --directed directed_raygentop_bilin.vh --random 1000 --note 'No reset port: the module flushes through the lead-in. The steps before the impulse are real zeros (defect F2 / D40 fixed).'

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dgul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dgvl}, result → resg
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dbvl}, result → resb
  • drul/drvl/dgul/dgvl/dbul/dbvl are new 7-bit stage-1 registers holding ru-rw, rv-rw, gu-gw, gv-gw, bu-bw, bv-bw. No product-term port is tied to 0 (all four are used on every block).

PLAN:

Why all nine products fit in three blocks. All of i1r…i3b are internal regs read only by the three assign statements at lines 2884–2886 (checked: no other reader in the file; the only instantiation of bilinearintrp is line 2012 and it connects only r, g, b). Each output is 7 bits, so only the sum mod 2^7 is visible. Line 2862 gives wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v at the same edge as ul <= u and vl <= v (lines 2863–2864), so wl ≡ -(1 + ul + vl) (mod 128) exactly, for all values, including the mod-256 wrap. Therefore

r = ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl            (mod 128)
  = ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl   (mod 128)

and the same for g and b. That is two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 per colour — plus a 7-bit correction term - rwl outside the block. This is exact arithmetic, not an approximation: no ASSUMES line is needed.

Edit to make (replace the declarations at lines 2794–2816, the always block body at 2860–2883, and the assigns at 2884–2886; leave the u/v case statement at 2818–2858 and the port list untouched):

verilog
        reg[7:0] u;
        reg[7:0] v;
        reg[7:0] ul;
        reg[7:0] vl;
        reg[6:0] rwl, gwl, bwl;          // stage 1 of the rw/gw/bw delay
        reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;       // stage 2, aligns with the block result
        reg[6:0] drul, drvl, dgul, dgvl, dbul, dbvl;
        reg[6:0] sr, sg, sb;             // registered block results, low 7 bits
        wire[63:0] resr, resg, resb;

        comb_sop_2_18 sop_r (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, drul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, drvl}), .result(resr));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_g (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dgul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dgvl}), .result(resg));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_b (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dbul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dbvl}), .result(resb));

        // (the u/v case statement of lines 2818-2858 stays exactly as it is)

        always @(posedge clk)
        begin
           ul <= u;
           vl <= v;
           rwl <= rw;   gwl <= gw;   bwl <= bw;
           rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl;
           drul <= ru - rw;   drvl <= rv - rw;
           dgul <= gu - gw;   dgvl <= gv - gw;
           dbul <= bu - bw;   dbvl <= bv - bw;
           sr <= resr[6:0];
           sg <= resg[6:0];
           sb <= resb[6:0];
        end

        assign r = sr - rwl2;
        assign g = sg - gwl2;
        assign b = sb - bwl2;

Deleted as dead: wl, i1r–i3b, and rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (their only readers were the nine multiplies). Keep the module header, port list, and the u/v combinational case block byte-for-byte.

Width/wrap check. ul ≤ 255 (8 bits ≤ ax's 18, zero-extended); drul ≤ 127 (7 bits ≤ ay's 19, zero-extended). Each product ≤ 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^64, so the block never wraps and resr[6:0] is the true sum mod 128. sr - rwl2 is 7-bit unsigned arithmetic, wrapping mod 128, which is what the identity requires. The original assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) evaluates in 15 bits and truncates to 7, i.e. also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Cycle alignment. Original: r(t) = ul(t-1)*rul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*rvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*rwl(t-1), i.e. a function of the module inputs at t-2. New: sr(t) = resr(t-1) = ul(t-1)*drul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*drvl(t-1) with drul(t-1) = (ru-rw)(t-2), and rwl2(t) = rw(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle.

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,rul,rvl,wl,rwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input add → output. Two register stages from operand to result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,drul,drvl, and the rwl leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 1 register (sr/sg/sb, and rwl2 in parallel) → combinational 7-bit subtract → output. Still two register stages from operand to result; the product register became the sum register, and the operand subtracts were pushed in front of the stage-1 register so the block sees register outputs directly (path ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 ns).

Candidates left unconverted: none. The six ul*/vl* products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) map directly onto the blocks' two product terms. The three wl* products (2876, 2879, 2882) are not computed as multiplies any more: substituting wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) folds each one into its sum's other two products (as the -rwl offset in the operands) plus the single 7-bit - rwl2 correction outside the block, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Reset. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none (reset policy: none). The added registers drul…dbvl, rwl2/gwl2/bwl2, sr/sg/sb start unknown; all of them are written on the first posedge and the compare only begins after the 8-cycle lead-in, during which the inputs are held at zero, so r=g=b=0 matches the original from the first compared cycle onward. Nothing relies on their initial value.

Area/delay note for the editor (no action needed): 3 DSP tiles are added (761 337 MWTA) and nine 8×7 soft multipliers plus nine 15-bit and six 7-bit registers are removed; the output path shortens from a 15-bit 3-input add to a 7-bit subtract.

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

APPROVE

Verified against /work/raygentop.v (module bilinearintrp, lines 2762–2887), sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v and sim_reference/tb_equiv_bilin.v:

  • Visibility. i1r…i3b, rul…bwl, wl, ul, vl appear only inside 2798–2886 (grep over the whole file); the module's only instantiation is line 2012, which takes just r, g, b. Outputs are 7 bits (2786–2791), so only the sums mod 2^7 are observable, as the plan claims.
  • The folding identity. Line 2862 wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v is an 8-bit assignment, so wl ≡ 255 - u - v (mod 256), and since 128 | 256 also wl ≡ -1-ul-vl (mod 128) with ul <= u, vl <= v at the same edge (2863–2864). Hence ul*rul+vl*rvl+wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl)+vl*(rvl-rwl)-rwl (mod 128) exactly, including the mod-256 wrap cases the directed vectors exercise (directed_raygentop_bilin.vh:16,19: u=255,v=200→wl=56≡-456; u=200,v=200→wl=111≡-401). No ASSUMES is needed, correctly.
  • Widths/wrap. assign r (2884) evaluates the 15-bit sum and truncates to 7 → mod 128, and 128 divides 2^15, so the original's 15-bit wrap is invisible. Block operands {10'b0,ul}=18, {12'b0,drul}=19 match comb_sop_2_18's ax[17:0]/ay[18:0]; max sum 2·255·127 = 64770, no 64-bit wrap, so resr[6:0] is the exact sum mod 128.
  • Timing. drul(t)=ru(t-1)-rw(t-1)=rul(t)-rwl(t), so sr(t)=resr(t-1) reproduces i1r(t)+i2r(t), and rwl2(t)=rwl(t-1) supplies the correction with the right delay. Two register stages operand→result before and after; one stage still sits between the products and the final add.
  • Startup. Added regs start unknown, but all are written unconditionally and are defined and zero by the second posedge; tb_equiv_bilin.v:216–219 holds all inputs at 0 for LEAD_IN=8 cycles before cmp=1, so no X reaches a compared cycle. With the rule's all-zero original init, cycles 0–1 also match (the inconsistent wl=0 is multiplied by rwl=0). No reset port exists and the testbench drives none (reset policy: none).
  • Required items. All nine candidates accounted for (none left unconverted, with the wl-product treatment explained), 3 blocks stated, register stages before and after stated.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit raygentop.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356. The plan names their shared sum as 'the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh raygentop.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dgul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dgvl}, result → resg
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dbvl}, result → resb
  • drul/drvl/dgul/dgvl/dbul/dbvl are new 7-bit stage-1 registers holding ru-rw, rv-rw, gu-gw, gv-gw, bu-bw, bv-bw. No product-term port is tied to 0 (all four are used on every block).

PLAN:

Why all nine products fit in three blocks. All of i1r…i3b are internal regs read only by the three assign statements at lines 2884–2886 (checked: no other reader in the file; the only instantiation of bilinearintrp is line 2012 and it connects only r, g, b). Each output is 7 bits, so only the sum mod 2^7 is visible. Line 2862 gives wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v at the same edge as ul <= u and vl <= v (lines 2863–2864), so wl ≡ -(1 + ul + vl) (mod 128) exactly, for all values, including the mod-256 wrap. Therefore

r = ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl            (mod 128)
  = ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl   (mod 128)

and the same for g and b. That is two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 per colour — plus a 7-bit correction term - rwl outside the block. This is exact arithmetic, not an approximation: no ASSUMES line is needed.

Edit to make (replace the declarations at lines 2794–2816, the always block body at 2860–2883, and the assigns at 2884–2886; leave the u/v case statement at 2818–2858 and the port list untouched):

verilog
        reg[7:0] u;
        reg[7:0] v;
        reg[7:0] ul;
        reg[7:0] vl;
        reg[6:0] rwl, gwl, bwl;          // stage 1 of the rw/gw/bw delay
        reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;       // stage 2, aligns with the block result
        reg[6:0] drul, drvl, dgul, dgvl, dbul, dbvl;
        reg[6:0] sr, sg, sb;             // registered block results, low 7 bits
        wire[63:0] resr, resg, resb;

        comb_sop_2_18 sop_r (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, drul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, drvl}), .result(resr));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_g (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dgul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dgvl}), .result(resg));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_b (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dbul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dbvl}), .result(resb));

        // (the u/v case statement of lines 2818-2858 stays exactly as it is)

        always @(posedge clk)
        begin
           ul <= u;
           vl <= v;
           rwl <= rw;   gwl <= gw;   bwl <= bw;
           rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl;
           drul <= ru - rw;   drvl <= rv - rw;
           dgul <= gu - gw;   dgvl <= gv - gw;
           dbul <= bu - bw;   dbvl <= bv - bw;
           sr <= resr[6:0];
           sg <= resg[6:0];
           sb <= resb[6:0];
        end

        assign r = sr - rwl2;
        assign g = sg - gwl2;
        assign b = sb - bwl2;

Deleted as dead: wl, i1r–i3b, and rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (their only readers were the nine multiplies). Keep the module header, port list, and the u/v combinational case block byte-for-byte.

Width/wrap check. ul ≤ 255 (8 bits ≤ ax's 18, zero-extended); drul ≤ 127 (7 bits ≤ ay's 19, zero-extended). Each product ≤ 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^64, so the block never wraps and resr[6:0] is the true sum mod 128. sr - rwl2 is 7-bit unsigned arithmetic, wrapping mod 128, which is what the identity requires. The original assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) evaluates in 15 bits and truncates to 7, i.e. also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Cycle alignment. Original: r(t) = ul(t-1)*rul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*rvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*rwl(t-1), i.e. a function of the module inputs at t-2. New: sr(t) = resr(t-1) = ul(t-1)*drul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*drvl(t-1) with drul(t-1) = (ru-rw)(t-2), and rwl2(t) = rw(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle.

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,rul,rvl,wl,rwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input add → output. Two register stages from operand to result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,drul,drvl, and the rwl leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 1 register (sr/sg/sb, and rwl2 in parallel) → combinational 7-bit subtract → output. Still two register stages from operand to result; the product register became the sum register, and the operand subtracts were pushed in front of the stage-1 register so the block sees register outputs directly (path ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 ns).

Candidates left unconverted: none. The six ul*/vl* products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) map directly onto the blocks' two product terms. The three wl* products (2876, 2879, 2882) are not computed as multiplies any more: substituting wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) folds each one into its sum's other two products (as the -rwl offset in the operands) plus the single 7-bit - rwl2 correction outside the block, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Reset. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none (reset policy: none). The added registers drul…dbvl, rwl2/gwl2/bwl2, sr/sg/sb start unknown; all of them are written on the first posedge and the compare only begins after the 8-cycle lead-in, during which the inputs are held at zero, so r=g=b=0 matches the original from the first compared cycle onward. Nothing relies on their initial value.

Area/delay note for the editor (no action needed): 3 DSP tiles are added (761 337 MWTA) and nine 8×7 soft multipliers plus nine 15-bit and six 7-bit registers are removed; the output path shortens from a 15-bit 3-input add to a 7-bit subtract.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

What I changed, region by region

All nine regions are in bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762). They collapse into three comb_sop_2_18 instances, one per colour sum, exactly as the plan specifies.

Regions $mul$2874 (ul*rul) and $mul$2875 (vl*rvl) → the two product terms of sop_r: ax={10'b0,ul}, ay={12'b0,drul}, bx={10'b0,vl}, by={12'b0,drvl}, result(resr). No port tied to 0.

Regions $mul$2877, $mul$2878 → sop_g with dgul/dgvl; $mul$2880, $mul$2881 → sop_b with dbul/dbvl. Same shape.

Regions $mul$2876, $mul$2879, $mul$2882 (the wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl terms) are no longer multiplies. Line 2862 registered wl <= 255-u-v on the same edge as ul<=u, vl<=v, so wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) exactly, including the mod-256 wrap. Since the outputs are 7 bits, each term folds into its sum's other two products as a -rwl offset on the operands, plus one 7-bit correction outside the block. No ASSUMES line is needed — this is exact, not a bound.

Concrete edits
  • Declarations (2794–2816): removed wl, i1r–i3b, and rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (dead once the multiplies are gone — I checked, their only readers were lines 2874–2886, and the sole instantiation of bilinearintrp at line 2012 connects only r, g, b). Added rwl2/gwl2/bwl2, drul/drvl/dgul/dgvl/dbul/dbvl, sr/sg/sb, and wire[63:0] resr/resg/resb; kept rwl/gwl/bwl.
  • Always block (2860–2883): stage 1 now registers ul, vl, the rwl/gwl/bwl leg and the six operand differences (ru-rw etc., pushed in front of the register so the block sees register outputs directly); stage 2 registers resr[6:0]/resg[6:0]/resb[6:0] into sr/sg/sb and rwl→rwl2 in parallel.
  • Assigns (2884–2886): assign r = (sr - rwl2); and likewise for g, b — 7-bit unsigned subtract, wrapping mod 128, matching the original's truncation of the 15-bit 3-input add.
  • The u/v case statement (2818–2858), the module header and the port list are untouched.

Timing and widths behave as planned: ul ≤ 255 and drul ≤ 127 zero-extend into the 18/19-bit ports, the block sum is at most 64770 so result never wraps, and the register depth from module input to output is still two stages (the product register became the sum register).

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file raygentop.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dgul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dgvl}, result → resg
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dbvl}, result → resb
  • drul/drvl/dgul/dgvl/dbul/dbvl are new 7-bit stage-1 registers holding ru-rw, rv-rw, gu-gw, gv-gw, bu-bw, bv-bw. No product-term port is tied to 0 (all four are used on every block).

PLAN:

Why all nine products fit in three blocks. All of i1r…i3b are internal regs read only by the three assign statements at lines 2884–2886 (checked: no other reader in the file; the only instantiation of bilinearintrp is line 2012 and it connects only r, g, b). Each output is 7 bits, so only the sum mod 2^7 is visible. Line 2862 gives wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v at the same edge as ul <= u and vl <= v (lines 2863–2864), so wl ≡ -(1 + ul + vl) (mod 128) exactly, for all values, including the mod-256 wrap. Therefore

r = ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl            (mod 128)
  = ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl   (mod 128)

and the same for g and b. That is two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 per colour — plus a 7-bit correction term - rwl outside the block. This is exact arithmetic, not an approximation: no ASSUMES line is needed.

Edit to make (replace the declarations at lines 2794–2816, the always block body at 2860–2883, and the assigns at 2884–2886; leave the u/v case statement at 2818–2858 and the port list untouched):

verilog
        reg[7:0] u;
        reg[7:0] v;
        reg[7:0] ul;
        reg[7:0] vl;
        reg[6:0] rwl, gwl, bwl;          // stage 1 of the rw/gw/bw delay
        reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;       // stage 2, aligns with the block result
        reg[6:0] drul, drvl, dgul, dgvl, dbul, dbvl;
        reg[6:0] sr, sg, sb;             // registered block results, low 7 bits
        wire[63:0] resr, resg, resb;

        comb_sop_2_18 sop_r (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, drul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, drvl}), .result(resr));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_g (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dgul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dgvl}), .result(resg));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_b (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dbul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dbvl}), .result(resb));

        // (the u/v case statement of lines 2818-2858 stays exactly as it is)

        always @(posedge clk)
        begin
           ul <= u;
           vl <= v;
           rwl <= rw;   gwl <= gw;   bwl <= bw;
           rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl;
           drul <= ru - rw;   drvl <= rv - rw;
           dgul <= gu - gw;   dgvl <= gv - gw;
           dbul <= bu - bw;   dbvl <= bv - bw;
           sr <= resr[6:0];
           sg <= resg[6:0];
           sb <= resb[6:0];
        end

        assign r = sr - rwl2;
        assign g = sg - gwl2;
        assign b = sb - bwl2;

Deleted as dead: wl, i1r–i3b, and rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (their only readers were the nine multiplies). Keep the module header, port list, and the u/v combinational case block byte-for-byte.

Width/wrap check. ul ≤ 255 (8 bits ≤ ax's 18, zero-extended); drul ≤ 127 (7 bits ≤ ay's 19, zero-extended). Each product ≤ 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^64, so the block never wraps and resr[6:0] is the true sum mod 128. sr - rwl2 is 7-bit unsigned arithmetic, wrapping mod 128, which is what the identity requires. The original assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) evaluates in 15 bits and truncates to 7, i.e. also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Cycle alignment. Original: r(t) = ul(t-1)*rul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*rvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*rwl(t-1), i.e. a function of the module inputs at t-2. New: sr(t) = resr(t-1) = ul(t-1)*drul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*drvl(t-1) with drul(t-1) = (ru-rw)(t-2), and rwl2(t) = rw(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle.

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,rul,rvl,wl,rwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input add → output. Two register stages from operand to result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,drul,drvl, and the rwl leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 1 register (sr/sg/sb, and rwl2 in parallel) → combinational 7-bit subtract → output. Still two register stages from operand to result; the product register became the sum register, and the operand subtracts were pushed in front of the stage-1 register so the block sees register outputs directly (path ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 ns).

Candidates left unconverted: none. The six ul*/vl* products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) map directly onto the blocks' two product terms. The three wl* products (2876, 2879, 2882) are not computed as multiplies any more: substituting wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) folds each one into its sum's other two products (as the -rwl offset in the operands) plus the single 7-bit - rwl2 correction outside the block, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Reset. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none (reset policy: none). The added registers drul…dbvl, rwl2/gwl2/bwl2, sr/sg/sb start unknown; all of them are written on the first posedge and the compare only begins after the 8-cycle lead-in, during which the inputs are held at zero, so r=g=b=0 matches the original from the first compared cycle onward. Nothing relies on their initial value.

Area/delay note for the editor (no action needed): 3 DSP tiles are added (761 337 MWTA) and nine 8×7 soft multipliers plus nine 15-bit and six 7-bit registers are removed; the output path shortens from a 15-bit 3-input add to a 7-bit subtract.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance. The edited bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2794–2888) matches the approved plan text essentially verbatim: the declaration block, the three comb_sop_2_18 instances, the rewritten always @(posedge clk) body, and the three assigns. The u/v case statement (2824–2864) and the module header/port list are untouched, and the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms nothing outside bilinearintrp changed. No change falls outside the regions the plan names.

Ports. All three instances connect all four product-term ports plus result by name: ax={10'b0,ul} (8+10=18), ay={12'b0,drul} (7+12=19), bx={10'b0,vl}, by={12'b0,drvl}, widths exact, zero-extension explicit. Nothing is tied to 0, consistent with the plan. resr/resg/resb drive only sr/sg/sb (2882–2884), as planned. 3 DSP tiles of 132.

Same behavior. wl <= 8'hFF - u - v wrapped mod 256, so wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) for all inputs; r is 7 bits, so only the sum mod 128 is visible. Hence ul*rul+vl*rvl+wl*rwl ≡ ul*(ru-rw)+vl*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128), exactly what sr - rwl2 computes — drul <= ru - rw wraps mod 128 in its 7-bit target, and assign r = sr - rwl2 is 7-bit context. The original i1r..i3r (15-bit) never truncate a product (255·127=32385), and the 3-input add's mod-2^15 wrap is irrelevant after truncation to 7 bits, so regrouping is exact, not approximate. No ASSUMES line is needed, and none is present.

Block wrap. Max product 255·127=32385, sum ≤ 64770, far below 2^64; resr[6:0] is the true sum mod 128. Contract's combinational, same-cycle semantics match the model in sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v.

Visibility. The deleted regs (wl, i1r…i3b, rul/rvl/gul/gvl/bul/bvl) had no reader outside the nine deleted multiplies; grep finds no other use, and the sole instantiation (raygentop.v:2012) connects only r,g,b → blr/blg/blb. Nothing internal was hidden inside a block.

Terms. All nine original products are accounted for: six map onto the blocks' product terms, the three wl* products are folded via the exact mod-128 identity into the operand offsets plus the single - rwl2/- gwl2/- bwl2 correction outside the block.

Registers/latency/reset. Pipeline depth is preserved on both legs: ru → drul → block → sr → assign and rw → rwl → rwl2 → assign, both two register stages, matching the original's two. The module has no reset (policy none); added registers start unknown but are all written on the first posedge and nothing reads their initial value. Sim reports x on dut only: 0, 0 mismatches over 1052 cycles.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14

16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

prompt

4 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

id
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

The plan whose result is measured above

The measured result above is first pass's. raygentop.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which your plan rewrites afresh; first pass is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The plan whose measured result is shown

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dgul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dgvl}, result → resg
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dbvl}, result → resb
  • drul/drvl/dgul/dgvl/dbul/dbvl are new 7-bit stage-1 registers holding ru-rw, rv-rw, gu-gw, gv-gw, bu-bw, bv-bw. No product-term port is tied to 0 (all four are used on every block).

PLAN:

Why all nine products fit in three blocks. All of i1r…i3b are internal regs read only by the three assign statements at lines 2884–2886 (checked: no other reader in the file; the only instantiation of bilinearintrp is line 2012 and it connects only r, g, b). Each output is 7 bits, so only the sum mod 2^7 is visible. Line 2862 gives wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v at the same edge as ul <= u and vl <= v (lines 2863–2864), so wl ≡ -(1 + ul + vl) (mod 128) exactly, for all values, including the mod-256 wrap. Therefore

r = ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl            (mod 128)
  = ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl   (mod 128)

and the same for g and b. That is two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 per colour — plus a 7-bit correction term - rwl outside the block. This is exact arithmetic, not an approximation: no ASSUMES line is needed.

Edit to make (replace the declarations at lines 2794–2816, the always block body at 2860–2883, and the assigns at 2884–2886; leave the u/v case statement at 2818–2858 and the port list untouched):

verilog
        reg[7:0] u;
        reg[7:0] v;
        reg[7:0] ul;
        reg[7:0] vl;
        reg[6:0] rwl, gwl, bwl;          // stage 1 of the rw/gw/bw delay
        reg[6:0] rwl2, gwl2, bwl2;       // stage 2, aligns with the block result
        reg[6:0] drul, drvl, dgul, dgvl, dbul, dbvl;
        reg[6:0] sr, sg, sb;             // registered block results, low 7 bits
        wire[63:0] resr, resg, resb;

        comb_sop_2_18 sop_r (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, drul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, drvl}), .result(resr));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_g (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dgul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dgvl}), .result(resg));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_b (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dbul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dbvl}), .result(resb));

        // (the u/v case statement of lines 2818-2858 stays exactly as it is)

        always @(posedge clk)
        begin
           ul <= u;
           vl <= v;
           rwl <= rw;   gwl <= gw;   bwl <= bw;
           rwl2 <= rwl; gwl2 <= gwl; bwl2 <= bwl;
           drul <= ru - rw;   drvl <= rv - rw;
           dgul <= gu - gw;   dgvl <= gv - gw;
           dbul <= bu - bw;   dbvl <= bv - bw;
           sr <= resr[6:0];
           sg <= resg[6:0];
           sb <= resb[6:0];
        end

        assign r = sr - rwl2;
        assign g = sg - gwl2;
        assign b = sb - bwl2;

Deleted as dead: wl, i1r–i3b, and rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl (their only readers were the nine multiplies). Keep the module header, port list, and the u/v combinational case block byte-for-byte.

Width/wrap check. ul ≤ 255 (8 bits ≤ ax's 18, zero-extended); drul ≤ 127 (7 bits ≤ ay's 19, zero-extended). Each product ≤ 32385, the block sum ≤ 64770 — far below 2^64, so the block never wraps and resr[6:0] is the true sum mod 128. sr - rwl2 is 7-bit unsigned arithmetic, wrapping mod 128, which is what the identity requires. The original assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) evaluates in 15 bits and truncates to 7, i.e. also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Cycle alignment. Original: r(t) = ul(t-1)*rul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*rvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*rwl(t-1), i.e. a function of the module inputs at t-2. New: sr(t) = resr(t-1) = ul(t-1)*drul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*drvl(t-1) with drul(t-1) = (ru-rw)(t-2), and rwl2(t) = rw(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle.

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,rul,rvl,wl,rwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input add → output. Two register stages from operand to result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,drul,drvl, and the rwl leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 1 register (sr/sg/sb, and rwl2 in parallel) → combinational 7-bit subtract → output. Still two register stages from operand to result; the product register became the sum register, and the operand subtracts were pushed in front of the stage-1 register so the block sees register outputs directly (path ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 ns).

Candidates left unconverted: none. The six ul*/vl* products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) map directly onto the blocks' two product terms. The three wl* products (2876, 2879, 2882) are not computed as multiplies any more: substituting wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) folds each one into its sum's other two products (as the -rwl offset in the operands) plus the single 7-bit - rwl2 correction outside the block, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Reset. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none (reset policy: none). The added registers drul…dbvl, rwl2/gwl2/bwl2, sr/sg/sb start unknown; all of them are written on the first posedge and the compare only begins after the 8-cycle lead-in, during which the inputs are held at zero, so r=g=b=0 matches the original from the first compared cycle onward. Nothing relies on their initial value.

Area/delay note for the editor (no action needed): 3 DSP tiles are added (761 337 MWTA) and nine 8×7 soft multipliers plus nine 15-bit and six 7-bit registers are removed; the output path shortens from a 15-bit 3-input add to a 7-bit subtract.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dgul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dgvl}, result → resg (only resg[6:0] used)
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dbvl}, result → resb (only resb[6:0] used)
  • drul/drvl/dgul/dgvl/dbul/dbvl are 7-bit stage-1 registers holding ru-rw, rv-rw, gu-gw, gv-gw, bu-bw, bv-bw. All four product-term ports are used on every block; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

This is the same regrouping as the measured first pass, with the -rw correction moved from the output into the stage-2 register, which deletes the three 7-bit delay registers rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 (21 flip-flops) and the three output subtracters from the combinational output path. Everything else about the identity, the mapping and the port widths is unchanged and already verified by the reference simulation.

Why the measured pass did not win, and what is left to change. The seed-1 critical path reported for the first pass does not run through bilinearintrp at all: it starts at a flip-flop feeding a DSP multiply's b[3], costs 2.140 ns in the multiply and ends at $dff~32^Q~19, bit 19 of a 32-bit product register. That is matmult (raygentop.v:335-343, am11 <= Ax * m11 etc., 16×16 products registered into reg[31:0] am**, lines 322-330, with only bits [30:15] read at 348-350). matmult is not a candidate region, so no rewrite of bilinearintrp can shorten the critical path; the +2.12% period was routing/placement around unchanged logic, inside the stated 4.6% seed spread. DSP tiles were 6 in both the baseline and the rewrite, so the three blocks bought no DSP-area change either way, and the whole measured area delta was +27 900 ≈ one CLB. The only lever left in this module is soft-logic count, so this pass spends it: 21 fewer flip-flops and three fewer 7-bit subtracters on the output nets, with no added logic anywhere.

The identity (unchanged, exact, no ASSUMES needed). wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (line 2862) is clocked at the same edge as ul <= u, vl <= v (2863-2864), and 256 ≡ 0 (mod 128), so the 8-bit wrap is invisible mod 128 and wl ≡ -(1 + ul + vl) (mod 128). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits (2786-2791), so only the sum mod 128 is visible:

r = ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl  ≡  ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl   (mod 128)

and likewise for g, b. Two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 each — plus one 7-bit correction.

Visibility. wl, i1r…i3b, rul…bwl are regs local to bilinearintrp; grep over the whole file shows their only readers are lines 2874-2886. The module is instantiated once (line 2012) and only r, g, b leave it. So the regrouping is invisible outside, and nothing that goes inside a block is read elsewhere.

Edit to make. Replace the declarations at lines 2794-2816, the clocked block body at 2860-2883, and the assigns at 2884-2886. Leave the port list, the u/v combinational case statement (2818-2858) and the rest of the file byte-for-byte.

verilog

        reg[7:0] v;
