prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

id
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is revision 1. raygentop.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which the plan under review rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dgul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dgvl}, result → resg (only resg[6:0] used)
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, dbvl}, result → resb (only resb[6:0] used)
  • drul/drvl/dgul/dgvl/dbul/dbvl are 7-bit stage-1 registers holding ru-rw, rv-rw, gu-gw, gv-gw, bu-bw, bv-bw. All four product-term ports are used on every block; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

This is the same regrouping as the measured first pass, with the -rw correction moved from the output into the stage-2 register, which deletes the three 7-bit delay registers rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 (21 flip-flops) and the three output subtracters from the combinational output path. Everything else about the identity, the mapping and the port widths is unchanged and already verified by the reference simulation.

Why the measured pass did not win, and what is left to change. The seed-1 critical path reported for the first pass does not run through bilinearintrp at all: it starts at a flip-flop feeding a DSP multiply's b[3], costs 2.140 ns in the multiply and ends at $dff~32^Q~19, bit 19 of a 32-bit product register. That is matmult (raygentop.v:335-343, am11 <= Ax * m11 etc., 16×16 products registered into reg[31:0] am**, lines 322-330, with only bits [30:15] read at 348-350). matmult is not a candidate region, so no rewrite of bilinearintrp can shorten the critical path; the +2.12% period was routing/placement around unchanged logic, inside the stated 4.6% seed spread. DSP tiles were 6 in both the baseline and the rewrite, so the three blocks bought no DSP-area change either way, and the whole measured area delta was +27 900 ≈ one CLB. The only lever left in this module is soft-logic count, so this pass spends it: 21 fewer flip-flops and three fewer 7-bit subtracters on the output nets, with no added logic anywhere.

The identity (unchanged, exact, no ASSUMES needed). wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (line 2862) is clocked at the same edge as ul <= u, vl <= v (2863-2864), and 256 ≡ 0 (mod 128), so the 8-bit wrap is invisible mod 128 and wl ≡ -(1 + ul + vl) (mod 128). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits (2786-2791), so only the sum mod 128 is visible:

r = ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl  ≡  ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl   (mod 128)

and likewise for g, b. Two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 each — plus one 7-bit correction.

Visibility. wl, i1r…i3b, rul…bwl are regs local to bilinearintrp; grep over the whole file shows their only readers are lines 2874-2886. The module is instantiated once (line 2012) and only r, g, b leave it. So the regrouping is invisible outside, and nothing that goes inside a block is read elsewhere.

Edit to make. Replace the declarations at lines 2794-2816, the clocked block body at 2860-2883, and the assigns at 2884-2886. Leave the port list, the u/v combinational case statement (2818-2858) and the rest of the file byte-for-byte.

        always @(posedge clk)
        begin
           ul <= u;

           rwl <= rw;   gwl <= gw;   bwl <= bw;
           drul <= ru - rw;   drvl <= rv - rw;
           dgul <= gu - gw;   dgvl <= gv - gw;
           dbul <= bu - bw;   dbvl <= bv - bw;
           sr <= resr[6:0] - rwl;

        assign g = sg;
        assign b = sb;

Deleted as dead: wl, i1r–i3b, rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl, and (versus the first pass) rwl2, gwl2, bwl2.

Cycle alignment. Write x(t) for the value a register holds during cycle t, i.e. captured at the edge ending cycle t-1. Original: r(t) = ul(t-1)*rul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*rvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*rwl(t-1), a function of the module inputs of cycle t-2. New: sr(t) = resr(t-1) - rwl(t-1) with resr(t-1) = ul(t-1)*drul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*drvl(t-1), ul(t-1)=u(t-2), drul(t-1)=(ru-rw)(t-2), rwl(t-1)=rw(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle. The nonblocking sr <= resr[6:0] - rwl reads the old rwl, which is exactly rw(t-2) — this is what makes the extra rwl2 stage unnecessary.

Width, wrap and mod-128 check. ul ≤ 255 (8 bits into an 18-bit port, zero-extended); drul ≤ 127 (7 bits into a 19-bit port, zero-extended). Each product ≤ 32 385, the block sum ≤ 64 770, far below 2^64, so the block never wraps and resr[6:0] is the true two-product sum mod 128. ru - rw truncated to 7 bits is the true difference mod 128, and ul*((ru-rw) mod 128) ≡ ul*(ru-rw) (mod 128). resr[6:0] - rwl is 7-bit unsigned, wrapping mod 128, as the identity requires. The original (i1r+i2r+i3r) is evaluated in 15 bits and truncated to 7, also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,rul,rvl,wl,rwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input 15-bit add truncated to 7 bits → output. Two register stages operand-to-result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,drul,drvl, and the rwl leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 7-bit subtract → 1 register (sr/sg/sb) → output. Still two register stages operand-to-result; the product register became the sum register, the operand subtracts sit in front of stage 1 so the block sees register outputs directly (0.060 + route + 2.14 + a 7-bit subtract into setup, with large slack against the 4.85 ns matmult path), and the module outputs are now straight register outputs.

Candidates left unconverted: none. The six ul*/vl* products (2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) map onto the blocks' two product terms. The three wl* products (2876, 2879, 2882) are no longer computed as multiplies: substituting wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) folds each into its sum's other two products (the -rw inside the operands) plus the single 7-bit - rwl correction, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Reset. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none (reset policy: none). drul…dbvl, rwl/gwl/bwl and sr/sg/sb start unknown; every one is written on each posedge, and the inputs are held at zero through the 8-cycle lead-in before comparison starts, by which point sr=sg=sb=0, matching the original (wl=255, rwl=0, so the original's i3r is 0 too). Nothing relies on an initial value.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the period of this design is set by matmult (raygentop.v:335-350), not by bilinearintrp: a registered 16×16 multiply with ~2.7 ns of routing on a 2.14 ns DSP. Only exposing matmult's nine products as candidate regions (three sums of three 16×16 products, each truncated to bits [30:15]) could move the objective; with the candidate list restricted to bilinearintrp, a rewrite can change area by about one CLB and nothing else.

The plan

I'll read the source before deciding.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, vl}, ay = {12'b0, dgvu}, bx = {10'b0, wl}, by = {12'b0, dgwu}, result → resg (only resg[6:0] used)
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbub}, bx = {10'b0, wl}, by = {12'b0, dbwb}, result → resb (only resb[6:0] used)
  • stage-1 7-bit registers: drul=ru-rw, drvl=rv-rw, dgvu=gv-gu, dgwu=gw-gu, dbub=bu-bv, dbwb=bw-bv; corrections rwl=rw, gul=gu, bvl=bv; wl = 255-u-v as in the original source (line 2862). All four product-term ports are used on every block; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

This keeps the kept revision's structure (two products per colour in one block, one 7-bit correction subtract between the block output and the stage-2 register) and changes only which of the three equivalent mod-128 decompositions each colour uses, so that the two wide operand nets that feed the DSP ax/bx ports are no longer the same two registers on all three blocks.

Why this change and nothing else. The measured seed-1 path of revision 1 is entirely inside bilinearintrp and entirely routing-dominated: 0.060 (clk→Q) + 1.314 ns wire, FF → ax[0] + 2.140 (block) + 1.168 ns wire, result[0] → subtract + 0.214 (7-bit carry chain) + setup = 4.957. The two fixed terms (block 2.140, clk→Q 0.060) and the 7-bit chain are already minimal:

  • The chain cannot be removed. r ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128); the -rwl term is 1*rwl, a third product, and one block carries only two. Making it a third product would need a second block per colour (6 DSP tiles for this module instead of 3, +5% area each tile). Moving the subtract past the register instead was measured as the first pass: it cost ~4 CLBs (an extra 2-cycle delay of rw/gw/bw, 21 flip-flops, plus the subtracters landing on the module's output nets) and the period did not improve (4.950 vs 4.933), because the next path, in matmult, sits at about the same place. So revision 1's arrangement stays.
  • Block count cannot drop below 3: six products are needed and the operands cannot be packed two colours to a port (a product is up to 15 bits, ay/by are 19 and ax/bx 18, so a second 7-bit field would have to start at bit 15 and would not fit).
  • Area is already near the floor for this structure: per colour 2 difference registers + 1 correction register + 3 seven-bit subtracters, and the DSP tile count is 6 in both the baseline and the rewrite, so no tile can be freed.

That leaves the 2.48 ns of wire on the block path as the only term with slack. In revision 1 both ul and vl drive the ax and bx port of all three blocks, so each of those two nets has three DSP sinks spread across the DSP column and the placer must compromise. The identity below gives each colour a different pair of x-operands, cutting the fanout of every x net from 3 DSPs to 2, at a cost of one 8-bit register (wl, which the original design already had) and its 8-bit subtract.

The identity (exact mod 128, no ASSUMES needed). wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (line 2862) is clocked at the same edge as ul <= u, vl <= v (2863-2864), and 256 ≡ 0 (mod 128), so the 8-bit wrap is invisible mod 128 and wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl), equivalently ul ≡ -(1+vl+wl) and vl ≡ -(1+ul+wl). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits (2786-2791), so only the sums mod 128 are visible. Eliminating a different one of the three x-operands per colour:

r ≡ ul*(ru-rw) + vl*(rv-rw) - rw        (eliminate wl)   -> x-operands ul, vl
g ≡ vl*(gv-gu) + wl*(gw-gu) - gu        (eliminate ul)   -> x-operands vl, wl
b ≡ ul*(bu-bv) + wl*(bw-bv) - bv        (eliminate vl)   -> x-operands ul, wl

Fanout after this: ul → sop_r.ax, sop_b.ax; vl → sop_r.bx, sop_g.ax; wl → sop_g.bx, sop_b.bx. Two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 each — plus one 7-bit correction, as before.

Numeric spot-check of the two new forms (ul=3, vl=5, wl=247): green with gu=10, gv=20, gw=30: original (3*10+5*20+247*30) mod 128 = 116; new (5*10 + 247*20) mod 128 − 10 = 126 − 10 = 116 ✓. Blue with bu=100, bv=70, bw=90: original (3*100+5*70+247*90) mod 128 = 96; new (3*30 + 247*20) mod 128 − 70 = 38 − 70 mod 128 = 96 ✓.

Visibility. wl, i1r…i3b, rul…bwl are regs local to bilinearintrp; their only readers in the file are lines 2874-2886. The module is instantiated once (line 2012) and only r, g, b leave it, as blr/blg/blb, read at line 2011 and at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484. So nothing that goes inside a block is read elsewhere.

Edit to make. Replace the declarations at lines 2794-2816, the clocked block body at 2860-2883, and the assigns at 2884-2886. Leave the port list, the u/v combinational case statement (2818-2858) and the rest of the file byte-for-byte.

Deleted as dead: i1r–i3b, rul, rvl, gvl, gwl, bul, bwl and the other unused operand copies (only rwl, gul, bvl survive, as the three corrections).

Cycle alignment. Write x(t) for the value a register holds during cycle t. Original: g(t) = ul(t-1)*gul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*gvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*gwl(t-1), a function of the module inputs of cycle t-2. New: sg(t) = resg(t-1) - gul(t-1) with resg(t-1) = vl(t-1)*dgvu(t-1) + wl(t-1)*dgwu(t-1), and vl(t-1)=v(t-2), wl(t-1)=(255-u-v)(t-2), dgvu(t-1)=(gv-gu)(t-2), gul(t-1)=gu(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle. The nonblocking sg <= resg[6:0] - gul reads the old gul, which is exactly gu(t-2), so no extra delay stage is needed — the same mechanism verified in revision 1 with rwl.

Width, wrap and mod-128 check. ul, vl, wl ≤ 255 (8 bits into an 18-bit port, zero-extended); each difference is 7 bits into a 19-bit port, zero-extended. Each product ≤ 255*127 = 32 385, each block sum ≤ 64 770, far below 2^64, so no block ever wraps and res*[6:0] is the true two-product sum mod 128. A 7-bit difference such as gv-gu is the true difference mod 128, and vl*((gv-gu) mod 128) ≡ vl*(gv-gu) (mod 128); likewise wl is congruent mod 128 to the mathematical -(1+ul+vl) despite its 8-bit wrap. res*[6:0] - correction is 7-bit unsigned, wrapping mod 128, as the identity requires. The original (i1g+i2g+i3g) is evaluated in 15 bits and truncated to 7, also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Start-up. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none. Every register here is written unconditionally on every posedge and the pipeline is two deep, so from the third clock edge on the state is a pure function of the stimulus and identical in both designs; comparison starts after the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in. Nothing relies on an initial value. (With the inputs held at 0 during the lead-in, both designs give r=g=b=0: original wl=255, rwl=0; new resg = 0 + 255*0 = 0, sg = 0-0 = 0.)

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,wl,rul…bwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input 15-bit add truncated to 7 bits → output. Two register stages operand-to-result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,wl, the difference registers, and the correction leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 7-bit subtract → 1 register (sr/sg/sb) → output. Still two register stages operand-to-result; the product register became the sum register, the operand subtracts sit in front of stage 1 so the block sees register outputs directly, and the module outputs are straight register outputs.

Candidates left unconverted: none. All nine products are carried by the three blocks: the six ul*/vl*/wl* products that survive the substitution map onto the blocks' two product terms per colour, and the eliminated product of each colour (wl*rwl for red, ul*gul for green, vl*bvl for blue) is folded into the other two operands plus that colour's single 7-bit correction, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Honest expectation. This is a routing/fanout play on the one term of the critical path that still has slack; the delay model given to me does not price routing, so the gain cannot be predicted here. Area moves by about +8 flip-flops and +8 adder bits versus revision 1 (well under one CLB), DSP tiles stay at 3 for this module and 6 for the design. If the shared-net fanout was not what cost the 1.314 ns, this lands at parity with revision 1 and revision 1 remains the kept result.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the period floor of this design is set by matmult (raygentop.v:335-350), not by bilinearintrp: registered 16×16 multiplies with ~2.7 ns of routing around a 2.14 ns DSP, measured at 4.95 ns in the first pass where it was critical, against a 4.847 ns baseline. Only exposing matmult's nine products as candidate regions (three sums of three 16×16 products, each truncated to bits [30:15]) could move the objective materially; with the candidate list restricted to bilinearintrp, a rewrite can change area by a few CLBs and nothing else.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

APPROVE

Verification performed against /work/raygentop.v and sim_reference/:

Design claims — all confirmed.

  • Declarations at raygentop.v:2794-2816, case statement 2818-2858, clocked block 2860-2883, assigns 2884-2886, wl <= 255-u-v at 2862, ul/vl at 2863-2864 — all as cited.
  • Outputs r/g/b are 7-bit wires (2786-2791); bilinearintrp is instantiated exactly once (2012), driving blr/blg/blb, read only at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484.
  • Grep over the whole file: wl, i1r–i3b, rul–bwl appear only in their declarations and at 2862-2886. Nothing moved inside a block is read elsewhere, so Visibility holds.
  • matmult cite (335-350, 16×16 products into reg[31:0], bits [30:15] read) is accurate; it is a DESIGN_CHANGE note, not a correctness claim.

Same behavior. wl ≡ −(1+ul+vl) (mod 128) holds because 256 ≡ 0 (mod 128), and ul/vl/wl are stage-1 registers of the same u,v. The three decompositions each expand correctly:

  • red: ul(ru−rw)+vl(rv−rw)−rw = ul·ru+vl·rv−rw(1+ul+vl) ≡ ul·ru+vl·rv+wl·rw
  • green: vl(gv−gu)+wl(gw−gu)−gu, with 1+vl+wl ≡ −ul
  • blue: ul(bu−bv)+wl(bw−bv)−bv, with 1+ul+wl ≡ −vl

Both numeric spot-checks recompute correctly (green 116 = 116, blue 96 = 96). 7-bit truncation of each difference is congruent mod 128 and multiplication preserves that; the original's 15-bit product and 15-bit 3-input sum truncated to 7 bits is also mod 128 (32768 is a multiple of 128), so the regrouping is exact on everything visible.

Widths/wrap. {10'b0,ul}→18, {12'b0,d*}→19 match the port widths; max product 255·127 = 32385, block sum ≤ 64770, never near 2^64, so res*[6:0] is the true two-product sum mod 128. All four product ports used; none tied.

Cycle alignment. sr/sg/sb RHS is evaluated in the active region, so − rwl/gul/bvl reads the pre-edge value = rw/gu/bv(t−2) — the same cycle the block operands come from, matching the original's two-stage input → stage-1 regs → product reg → output. Output stays a function of inputs at t−2.

Registers/reset. No reset port; testbench policy "none", LEAD_IN 8 with all inputs zero and cmp enabled afterwards. Every new register is written unconditionally each posedge and the pipeline is two deep, so X clears by the second edge and both designs read 0 at the end of lead-in. Nothing relies on an initial value; no ASSUMES needed, and none is claimed.

Required items present. All nine candidates listed and converted ("Candidates left unconverted: none"); BLOCKS: 3; register stages before (2) and after (2) stated per converted region, with the one-stage product-to-sum relation accounted for.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);

Task

Edit raygentop.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356. The plan names their shared sum as 'the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh raygentop.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the source before deciding.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, vl}, ay = {12'b0, dgvu}, bx = {10'b0, wl}, by = {12'b0, dgwu}, result → resg (only resg[6:0] used)
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbub}, bx = {10'b0, wl}, by = {12'b0, dbwb}, result → resb (only resb[6:0] used)
  • stage-1 7-bit registers: drul=ru-rw, drvl=rv-rw, dgvu=gv-gu, dgwu=gw-gu, dbub=bu-bv, dbwb=bw-bv; corrections rwl=rw, gul=gu, bvl=bv; wl = 255-u-v as in the original source (line 2862). All four product-term ports are used on every block; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

This keeps the kept revision's structure (two products per colour in one block, one 7-bit correction subtract between the block output and the stage-2 register) and changes only which of the three equivalent mod-128 decompositions each colour uses, so that the two wide operand nets that feed the DSP ax/bx ports are no longer the same two registers on all three blocks.

Why this change and nothing else. The measured seed-1 path of revision 1 is entirely inside bilinearintrp and entirely routing-dominated: 0.060 (clk→Q) + 1.314 ns wire, FF → ax[0] + 2.140 (block) + 1.168 ns wire, result[0] → subtract + 0.214 (7-bit carry chain) + setup = 4.957. The two fixed terms (block 2.140, clk→Q 0.060) and the 7-bit chain are already minimal:

  • The chain cannot be removed. r ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128); the -rwl term is 1*rwl, a third product, and one block carries only two. Making it a third product would need a second block per colour (6 DSP tiles for this module instead of 3, +5% area each tile). Moving the subtract past the register instead was measured as the first pass: it cost ~4 CLBs (an extra 2-cycle delay of rw/gw/bw, 21 flip-flops, plus the subtracters landing on the module's output nets) and the period did not improve (4.950 vs 4.933), because the next path, in matmult, sits at about the same place. So revision 1's arrangement stays.
  • Block count cannot drop below 3: six products are needed and the operands cannot be packed two colours to a port (a product is up to 15 bits, ay/by are 19 and ax/bx 18, so a second 7-bit field would have to start at bit 15 and would not fit).
  • Area is already near the floor for this structure: per colour 2 difference registers + 1 correction register + 3 seven-bit subtracters, and the DSP tile count is 6 in both the baseline and the rewrite, so no tile can be freed.

That leaves the 2.48 ns of wire on the block path as the only term with slack. In revision 1 both ul and vl drive the ax and bx port of all three blocks, so each of those two nets has three DSP sinks spread across the DSP column and the placer must compromise. The identity below gives each colour a different pair of x-operands, cutting the fanout of every x net from 3 DSPs to 2, at a cost of one 8-bit register (wl, which the original design already had) and its 8-bit subtract.

The identity (exact mod 128, no ASSUMES needed). wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (line 2862) is clocked at the same edge as ul <= u, vl <= v (2863-2864), and 256 ≡ 0 (mod 128), so the 8-bit wrap is invisible mod 128 and wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl), equivalently ul ≡ -(1+vl+wl) and vl ≡ -(1+ul+wl). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits (2786-2791), so only the sums mod 128 are visible. Eliminating a different one of the three x-operands per colour:

r ≡ ul*(ru-rw) + vl*(rv-rw) - rw        (eliminate wl)   -> x-operands ul, vl
g ≡ vl*(gv-gu) + wl*(gw-gu) - gu        (eliminate ul)   -> x-operands vl, wl
b ≡ ul*(bu-bv) + wl*(bw-bv) - bv        (eliminate vl)   -> x-operands ul, wl

Fanout after this: ul → sop_r.ax, sop_b.ax; vl → sop_r.bx, sop_g.ax; wl → sop_g.bx, sop_b.bx. Two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 each — plus one 7-bit correction, as before.

Numeric spot-check of the two new forms (ul=3, vl=5, wl=247): green with gu=10, gv=20, gw=30: original (3*10+5*20+247*30) mod 128 = 116; new (5*10 + 247*20) mod 128 − 10 = 126 − 10 = 116 ✓. Blue with bu=100, bv=70, bw=90: original (3*100+5*70+247*90) mod 128 = 96; new (3*30 + 247*20) mod 128 − 70 = 38 − 70 mod 128 = 96 ✓.

Visibility. wl, i1r…i3b, rul…bwl are regs local to bilinearintrp; their only readers in the file are lines 2874-2886. The module is instantiated once (line 2012) and only r, g, b leave it, as blr/blg/blb, read at line 2011 and at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484. So nothing that goes inside a block is read elsewhere.

Edit to make. Replace the declarations at lines 2794-2816, the clocked block body at 2860-2883, and the assigns at 2884-2886. Leave the port list, the u/v combinational case statement (2818-2858) and the rest of the file byte-for-byte.

verilog
        reg[7:0] u;
        reg[7:0] v;
        reg[7:0] ul;
        reg[7:0] vl;
        reg[7:0] wl;
        reg[6:0] rwl, gul, bvl;
        reg[6:0] drul, drvl, dgvu, dgwu, dbub, dbwb;
        reg[6:0] sr, sg, sb;
        wire[63:0] resr, resg, resb;

        comb_sop_2_18 sop_r (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, drul}),
                             .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, drvl}), .result(resr));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_g (.ax({10'b0, vl}), .ay({12'b0, dgvu}),
                             .bx({10'b0, wl}), .by({12'b0, dgwu}), .result(resg));
        comb_sop_2_18 sop_b (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dbub}),
                             .bx({10'b0, wl}), .by({12'b0, dbwb}), .result(resb));

        always @(posedge clk)
        begin
           wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v ;
           ul <= u ;
           vl <= v ;
           rwl <= rw ;   gul <= gu ;   bvl <= bv ;
           drul <= ru - rw ;   drvl <= rv - rw ;
           dgvu <= gv - gu ;   dgwu <= gw - gu ;
           dbub <= bu - bv ;   dbwb <= bw - bv ;
           sr <= resr[6:0] - rwl ;
           sg <= resg[6:0] - gul ;
           sb <= resb[6:0] - bvl ;
        end

        assign r = sr ;
        assign g = sg ;
        assign b = sb ;

Deleted as dead: i1r–i3b, rul, rvl, gvl, gwl, bul, bwl and the other unused operand copies (only rwl, gul, bvl survive, as the three corrections).

Cycle alignment. Write x(t) for the value a register holds during cycle t. Original: g(t) = ul(t-1)*gul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*gvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*gwl(t-1), a function of the module inputs of cycle t-2. New: sg(t) = resg(t-1) - gul(t-1) with resg(t-1) = vl(t-1)*dgvu(t-1) + wl(t-1)*dgwu(t-1), and vl(t-1)=v(t-2), wl(t-1)=(255-u-v)(t-2), dgvu(t-1)=(gv-gu)(t-2), gul(t-1)=gu(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle. The nonblocking sg <= resg[6:0] - gul reads the old gul, which is exactly gu(t-2), so no extra delay stage is needed — the same mechanism verified in revision 1 with rwl.

Width, wrap and mod-128 check. ul, vl, wl ≤ 255 (8 bits into an 18-bit port, zero-extended); each difference is 7 bits into a 19-bit port, zero-extended. Each product ≤ 255*127 = 32 385, each block sum ≤ 64 770, far below 2^64, so no block ever wraps and res*[6:0] is the true two-product sum mod 128. A 7-bit difference such as gv-gu is the true difference mod 128, and vl*((gv-gu) mod 128) ≡ vl*(gv-gu) (mod 128); likewise wl is congruent mod 128 to the mathematical -(1+ul+vl) despite its 8-bit wrap. res*[6:0] - correction is 7-bit unsigned, wrapping mod 128, as the identity requires. The original (i1g+i2g+i3g) is evaluated in 15 bits and truncated to 7, also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Start-up. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none. Every register here is written unconditionally on every posedge and the pipeline is two deep, so from the third clock edge on the state is a pure function of the stimulus and identical in both designs; comparison starts after the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in. Nothing relies on an initial value. (With the inputs held at 0 during the lead-in, both designs give r=g=b=0: original wl=255, rwl=0; new resg = 0 + 255*0 = 0, sg = 0-0 = 0.)

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,wl,rul…bwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input 15-bit add truncated to 7 bits → output. Two register stages operand-to-result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,wl, the difference registers, and the correction leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 7-bit subtract → 1 register (sr/sg/sb) → output. Still two register stages operand-to-result; the product register became the sum register, the operand subtracts sit in front of stage 1 so the block sees register outputs directly, and the module outputs are straight register outputs.

Candidates left unconverted: none. All nine products are carried by the three blocks: the six ul*/vl*/wl* products that survive the substitution map onto the blocks' two product terms per colour, and the eliminated product of each colour (wl*rwl for red, ul*gul for green, vl*bvl for blue) is folded into the other two operands plus that colour's single 7-bit correction, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Honest expectation. This is a routing/fanout play on the one term of the critical path that still has slack; the delay model given to me does not price routing, so the gain cannot be predicted here. Area moves by about +8 flip-flops and +8 adder bits versus revision 1 (well under one CLB), DSP tiles stay at 3 for this module and 6 for the design. If the shared-net fanout was not what cost the 1.314 ns, this lands at parity with revision 1 and revision 1 remains the kept result.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

What changed, region by region

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
3	# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
4	# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
5	# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
6	# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
7	# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
8	# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
9	# of the target block's name fails this check.
10	# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
11	# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
12	# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
13	# instantiation shows up with its instance count.

17	lib=""
18	[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
19	[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
20	target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
21	if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
22	  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
23	  exit 1
24	fi
25	out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
26	rc=$?
27	if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
28	  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
29	  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
30	  echo "$out" | tail -10
31	  exit 1
32	fi
33	echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
34	echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
35	echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
36	                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
37	                   on {on=0}
38	                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
39	  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.

The design

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll read the source before deciding.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three colour sums in module bilinearintrp — r (i1r+i2r+i3r, line 2884), g (line 2885), b (line 2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, drul}, bx = {10'b0, vl}, by = {12'b0, drvl}, result → resr (only resr[6:0] used)
  • block sop_g: ax = {10'b0, vl}, ay = {12'b0, dgvu}, bx = {10'b0, wl}, by = {12'b0, dgwu}, result → resg (only resg[6:0] used)
  • block sop_b: ax = {10'b0, ul}, ay = {12'b0, dbub}, bx = {10'b0, wl}, by = {12'b0, dbwb}, result → resb (only resb[6:0] used)
  • stage-1 7-bit registers: drul=ru-rw, drvl=rv-rw, dgvu=gv-gu, dgwu=gw-gu, dbub=bu-bv, dbwb=bw-bv; corrections rwl=rw, gul=gu, bvl=bv; wl = 255-u-v as in the original source (line 2862). All four product-term ports are used on every block; none is tied to 0.

PLAN:

This keeps the kept revision's structure (two products per colour in one block, one 7-bit correction subtract between the block output and the stage-2 register) and changes only which of the three equivalent mod-128 decompositions each colour uses, so that the two wide operand nets that feed the DSP ax/bx ports are no longer the same two registers on all three blocks.

Why this change and nothing else. The measured seed-1 path of revision 1 is entirely inside bilinearintrp and entirely routing-dominated: 0.060 (clk→Q) + 1.314 ns wire, FF → ax[0] + 2.140 (block) + 1.168 ns wire, result[0] → subtract + 0.214 (7-bit carry chain) + setup = 4.957. The two fixed terms (block 2.140, clk→Q 0.060) and the 7-bit chain are already minimal:

  • The chain cannot be removed. r ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128); the -rwl term is 1*rwl, a third product, and one block carries only two. Making it a third product would need a second block per colour (6 DSP tiles for this module instead of 3, +5% area each tile). Moving the subtract past the register instead was measured as the first pass: it cost ~4 CLBs (an extra 2-cycle delay of rw/gw/bw, 21 flip-flops, plus the subtracters landing on the module's output nets) and the period did not improve (4.950 vs 4.933), because the next path, in matmult, sits at about the same place. So revision 1's arrangement stays.
  • Block count cannot drop below 3: six products are needed and the operands cannot be packed two colours to a port (a product is up to 15 bits, ay/by are 19 and ax/bx 18, so a second 7-bit field would have to start at bit 15 and would not fit).
  • Area is already near the floor for this structure: per colour 2 difference registers + 1 correction register + 3 seven-bit subtracters, and the DSP tile count is 6 in both the baseline and the rewrite, so no tile can be freed.

That leaves the 2.48 ns of wire on the block path as the only term with slack. In revision 1 both ul and vl drive the ax and bx port of all three blocks, so each of those two nets has three DSP sinks spread across the DSP column and the placer must compromise. The identity below gives each colour a different pair of x-operands, cutting the fanout of every x net from 3 DSPs to 2, at a cost of one 8-bit register (wl, which the original design already had) and its 8-bit subtract.

The identity (exact mod 128, no ASSUMES needed). wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (line 2862) is clocked at the same edge as ul <= u, vl <= v (2863-2864), and 256 ≡ 0 (mod 128), so the 8-bit wrap is invisible mod 128 and wl ≡ -(1+ul+vl), equivalently ul ≡ -(1+vl+wl) and vl ≡ -(1+ul+wl). The outputs r, g, b are 7 bits (2786-2791), so only the sums mod 128 are visible. Eliminating a different one of the three x-operands per colour:

r ≡ ul*(ru-rw) + vl*(rv-rw) - rw        (eliminate wl)   -> x-operands ul, vl
g ≡ vl*(gv-gu) + wl*(gw-gu) - gu        (eliminate ul)   -> x-operands vl, wl
b ≡ ul*(bu-bv) + wl*(bw-bv) - bv        (eliminate vl)   -> x-operands ul, wl

Fanout after this: ul → sop_r.ax, sop_b.ax; vl → sop_r.bx, sop_g.ax; wl → sop_g.bx, sop_b.bx. Two products per colour — exactly one comb_sop_2_18 each — plus one 7-bit correction, as before.

Numeric spot-check of the two new forms (ul=3, vl=5, wl=247): green with gu=10, gv=20, gw=30: original (3*10+5*20+247*30) mod 128 = 116; new (5*10 + 247*20) mod 128 − 10 = 126 − 10 = 116 ✓. Blue with bu=100, bv=70, bw=90: original (3*100+5*70+247*90) mod 128 = 96; new (3*30 + 247*20) mod 128 − 70 = 38 − 70 mod 128 = 96 ✓.

Visibility. wl, i1r…i3b, rul…bwl are regs local to bilinearintrp; their only readers in the file are lines 2874-2886. The module is instantiated once (line 2012) and only r, g, b leave it, as blr/blg/blb, read at line 2011 and at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484. So nothing that goes inside a block is read elsewhere.

Edit to make. Replace the declarations at lines 2794-2816, the clocked block body at 2860-2883, and the assigns at 2884-2886. Leave the port list, the u/v combinational case statement (2818-2858) and the rest of the file byte-for-byte.

Deleted as dead: i1r–i3b, rul, rvl, gvl, gwl, bul, bwl and the other unused operand copies (only rwl, gul, bvl survive, as the three corrections).

Cycle alignment. Write x(t) for the value a register holds during cycle t. Original: g(t) = ul(t-1)*gul(t-1) + vl(t-1)*gvl(t-1) + wl(t-1)*gwl(t-1), a function of the module inputs of cycle t-2. New: sg(t) = resg(t-1) - gul(t-1) with resg(t-1) = vl(t-1)*dgvu(t-1) + wl(t-1)*dgwu(t-1), and vl(t-1)=v(t-2), wl(t-1)=(255-u-v)(t-2), dgvu(t-1)=(gv-gu)(t-2), gul(t-1)=gu(t-2). Same inputs, same cycle. The nonblocking sg <= resg[6:0] - gul reads the old gul, which is exactly gu(t-2), so no extra delay stage is needed — the same mechanism verified in revision 1 with rwl.

Width, wrap and mod-128 check. ul, vl, wl ≤ 255 (8 bits into an 18-bit port, zero-extended); each difference is 7 bits into a 19-bit port, zero-extended. Each product ≤ 255*127 = 32 385, each block sum ≤ 64 770, far below 2^64, so no block ever wraps and res*[6:0] is the true two-product sum mod 128. A 7-bit difference such as gv-gu is the true difference mod 128, and vl*((gv-gu) mod 128) ≡ vl*(gv-gu) (mod 128); likewise wl is congruent mod 128 to the mathematical -(1+ul+vl) despite its 8-bit wrap. res*[6:0] - correction is 7-bit unsigned, wrapping mod 128, as the identity requires. The original (i1g+i2g+i3g) is evaluated in 15 bits and truncated to 7, also mod 128, so regrouping the adds cannot change the result.

Start-up. The module has no reset port and the testbench drives none. Every register here is written unconditionally on every posedge and the pipeline is two deep, so from the third clock edge on the state is a pure function of the stimulus and identical in both designs; comparison starts after the testbench's 8-cycle lead-in. Nothing relies on an initial value. (With the inputs held at 0 during the lead-in, both designs give r=g=b=0: original wl=255, rwl=0; new resg = 0 + 255*0 = 0, sg = 0-0 = 0.)

Register stages, per converted region (all nine behave the same):

  • Before: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,wl,rul…bwl) → multiply → 1 register (i1r…i3b) → combinational 3-input 15-bit add truncated to 7 bits → output. Two register stages operand-to-result; one stage between each product and its sum.
  • After: module input → 1 register (ul,vl,wl, the difference registers, and the correction leg) → block (combinational, 2.14 ns) → 7-bit subtract → 1 register (sr/sg/sb) → output. Still two register stages operand-to-result; the product register became the sum register, the operand subtracts sit in front of stage 1 so the block sees register outputs directly, and the module outputs are straight register outputs.

Candidates left unconverted: none. All nine products are carried by the three blocks: the six ul*/vl*/wl* products that survive the substitution map onto the blocks' two product terms per colour, and the eliminated product of each colour (wl*rwl for red, ul*gul for green, vl*bvl for blue) is folded into the other two operands plus that colour's single 7-bit correction, so every term of the original arithmetic is still present.

Honest expectation. This is a routing/fanout play on the one term of the critical path that still has slack; the delay model given to me does not price routing, so the gain cannot be predicted here. Area moves by about +8 flip-flops and +8 adder bits versus revision 1 (well under one CLB), DSP tiles stay at 3 for this module and 6 for the design. If the shared-net fanout was not what cost the 1.314 ns, this lands at parity with revision 1 and revision 1 remains the kept result.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the period floor of this design is set by matmult (raygentop.v:335-350), not by bilinearintrp: registered 16×16 multiplies with ~2.7 ns of routing around a 2.14 ns DSP, measured at 4.95 ns in the first pass where it was critical, against a 4.847 ns baseline. Only exposing matmult's nine products as candidate regions (three sums of three 16×16 products, each truncated to bits [30:15]) could move the objective materially; with the candidate list restricted to bilinearintrp, a rewrite can change area by a few CLBs and nothing else.

+           dbub <= bu - bv ;
+           dbwb <= bw - bv ;
+           sr <= resr[6:0] - rwl ;
+           sg <= resg[6:0] - gul ;
+           sb <= resb[6:0] - bvl ;
+        end
+        assign r = sr ;
+        assign g = sg ;
+        assign b = sb ;

ACCEPT

Checks performed:

Plan conformance. The edit touches exactly the regions the plan names: the declaration block (raygentop.v:2794-2820), the clocked body (2864-2881) and the three assigns (2882-2884). sim_run.log:3 confirms the subtree check — outside bilinearintrp the candidate equals the base source. The u/v case statement (2822-2862) and the port list are unchanged. The only deviation from the plan text is cosmetic (one declaration per line instead of grouped).

Ports. Three comb_sop_2_18 instances, matching BLOCKS: 3 and the log's "instantiations in DUT: 3". Each connects all four product-term ports plus result by name, widths exact: {10'b0, ul} = 18, {12'b0, drul} = 19, result → 64-bit wire. No port tied to 0; none needed. Operand mapping is exactly the plan's (sop_r: ul/drul, vl/drvl; sop_g: vl/dgvu, wl/dgwu; sop_b: ul/dbub, wl/dbwb), and each res*[6:0] drives only the corresponding stage-2 subtract.

Terms / same behavior. wl keeps its original definition (2866) and is clocked with ul,vl, so wl ≡ 127-ul-vl ≡ -(1+ul+vl) (mod 128). With that: red ul*drul+vl*drvl-rwl ≡ ul*ru+vl*rv-rw*(ul+vl+1) ≡ ul*ru+vl*rv+wl*rw; green vl+wl+1 ≡ -ul, so the -gul term restores ul*gu; blue ul+wl+1 ≡ -vl, restoring vl*bv. All nine original products are accounted for. The original assign r = (i1r+i2r+i3r) truncates to 7 bits, i.e. mod 128, so the regrouping is exact — no ASSUMES is required, consistent with the plan. Max block sum is 255·127·2 = 64 770, so the 64-bit result never wraps and res*[6:0] is the true sum mod 128.

Pipelining. Operands and corrections are all written at the same edge; the nonblocking sr <= resr[6:0] - rwl reads the pre-edge rwl, which is sampled at the same edge as the d* registers feeding resr. Two register stages input-to-output, as in the original, so r/g/b are valid in the same cycle. Confirmed by the same-cycle (latency offset 0) comparison passing on all 1052 checked cycles with 0 mismatches and 0 X's, plus a matching impulse response.

Visibility. ul, vl, wl, the difference registers, the corrections and sr/sg/sb are local to bilinearintrp; grep over the file shows no reader outside lines 2866-2884 (the ul/vl at 2706-2756 are a different module's registers). Only r, g, b leave through the single instantiation at 2012.

Reset. No reset port exists and none is needed; every register is written unconditionally each edge, the pipeline is two deep, and the new sr/sg/sb initial values are not relied on (reset policy "none", 8-cycle lead-in excluded from comparison).

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

Objective loop

3 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 2
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -14.00% against Pass 2, min-gain 0.046 (baseline seed_figures (area x delay over the entry's seed runs)))
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 4.7578e+06 · critical path 4.84712 ns · wirelength 25664 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 2.30616e+07 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.60%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 4.60%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
